# Fixedpoint port completion checklist

This tracks completion of the whole `src/muz/` port in `pkg/muz/`, including live dependencies outside that directory. A working engine or green test is not a whole-source completion claim. Source algorithms, proofs, models, incremental behavior, diagnostics and explicit ownership must all be accounted for.

## How to follow progress

Each completed item needs source paths, implementation/test evidence and a commit in the progress log. Unchecked means incomplete or unproven. Preserve existing encodings, safeguards and test coverage. Exclude dead source only after checking live consumers. Keep commits small and frequent; use focused native tests. Use the user's filtered three-tag command below for periodic integration, excluding the three slow package groups. Keep the canceled unfiltered run stopped.

## full testing tags should be checked every few hours

This will exclude the very slow tests that take 30 minutes and run in about 4 minutes:
~~~
cd g3/pkg; go test -timeout=0 -count=1 -short -tags g3checked,g3debug,g3trace $( go list ./... | grep -v math/polynomial | grep -v util/lp |grep -v pkg/qe)
~~~

Current integrated frontier: finite queries apply source-ordered simplification, inlining, similarity and filter/join lowering, preserve explicitly requested auxiliary table kinds and fuse dead-column projection. Raw DOC rules accept explicit extensional finite inputs with independent snapshots and expression-query answers. Public acyclic mixed DOC/finite products now use actual sieve/table components, preserve source coarse emptiness and support generated queries, updates and independently owned query clones. Unary DOC and all-Bool finite ground rules populate query snapshots, making that bounded path reachable from parsed programs; broader mixed programs remain open. Bound/interval products reach public facts, queries, owning answers, clones and updates; widening supports recursive strata of arbitrary predicate count with up to two positive tails per rule, using source cycle-breaking order, per-occurrence delta variants, loop-end delta-bank transitions and source all-or-nothing replay. External/interval acyclic identity/join/ground programs share one LOAD bank, lazily cached constants and terminal STORE; unary rules now select numeral constants, filter repeated variables, apply bound interpreted guards with source fused omitted-variable projection, and project/permute bound heads including nullaries. Two-positive-tail rules now apply bound interpreted guards after source join projection, with mapped columns and fused removal of guard-only variables. Two-tail numeral constants compile through fixed-column equality filters and a separate head projection; repeated bound heads reuse existing copies before source self-join or projection/join duplication. External recursion, unbound/negative guards, repeated two-tail variables and broader external bodies remain separate. Both BMC backends now use requested slicing with original-rule proof conversion. PDR multicore minimization precedes convex generalization in source order. These bounded entries do not claim whole-unit completion.

The raw unary DOC/product join dependency (`b7f124251`) and bounded mixed-ground consumer (`581f5570e`) are integrated. Unseeded two-column Bool/BV inputs assemble actual singleton DOC/table products and union their components in source order. This preserves source correlation loss: T(true,1) union T(false,2), joined with D(1), permits both booleans; a single T(true,2) joined with D(1) has an empty matched DOC component. Both tuple/body orientations and Hash/Sparse/Lazy backings pass public and parsed-query tests, including ownership, updates and dense failure/retry. Ordinary acyclic mixed-target forwarding now uses the same actual product clone/filter/project/rename path (`49fcf6c9b`). Initial seeded inputs/heads and conflicting explicit kinds still use the prior fallback. The concrete product-by-product join dependency is integrated in `76ce892ed`, using actual DOC and table components with no full substitutes. Public acyclic two-product dispatch now uses that concrete join; broader singleton-cache combinations remain open; do not add unregistered finite-product(DOC) adapters.

Wider bound bodies now run query-local FilterRules (priority 2000) then SimpleJoins (1000) in `75e07c7e9`, preserving the compiler's two-positive-tail guard. A separate RuleManager over the same AST manager owns copied rules and fresh predicates; filter/split origin ledgers preserve both requested component kinds. A temporary activation clones original seeds and publishes only original results after successful cleanup. Raw rules, predicate order, seeds and requested metadata remain unchanged. Source prior-saturation setters have no callers, so no extra flag or row-statistics adapter was added. The source cost and retryable-cleanup prerequisites remain documented in `0173d85b6` and `7ade8a9bd`. Nonempty product negation and broader preprocessing combinations remain separate.


The DOC consumer now admits an acyclic mixed target with two inputs already known to have product kinds, using `UDocTableProduct.Join` then the existing head projection/rename/union path. Preserve distinct body variables, valid same-sort shared keys, unique bound head variables and unseeded target restrictions until their broader cases are implemented. Crucially, an absent product result after an earlier empty query can coexist with subsequently added ordinary finite seed facts: inspect that storage and report the explicit unsupported capability boundary for nonempty ordinary inputs; never silently treat those facts as empty. Primitive guards, mixed-kind source fallback, projected masks, self-joins and dense failure cleanup are already tested.


The whole `dl_engine_base.h` audit is complete and independently approved in [ENGINE_BASE_AUDIT.md](ENGINE_BASE_AUDIT.md), committed as `a760d78f3`. Every declaration, branch, default, owned local and actual consumer is mapped, including explicit Go adaptations. Its final live gap was unsupported Datalog certificate dispatch (`f07739846`). Only this header is promoted; derived engine algorithms and context/rule lifecycles keep separate obligations.

Acceleration tactics are durable for every batch:

- Select work from the dependency frontier: prefer a live source consumer whose dependencies already exist, and port two or more source units together when that is the smallest coherent Go design.
- Divide lanes by non-overlapping ownership, not arbitrary file counts. Use read-only source triage before editing shared infrastructure; the integration owner alone coordinates cross-lane files, the inventory and this checklist.
- Prioritize actual registered engine/transform/relation specializations and end-to-end query paths. Do not spend a lane on dead templates, unused adapters or diagnostic polish while a functional consumer remains open.
- Give each behavior a source-derived semantic oracle, ownership/lifecycle assertions and a focused native package test. Preserve guards and existing tests; record legacy source limitations explicitly instead of inventing silent divergences.
- Integrate and commit each green slice immediately with a one-sentence message, then reassign that lane from the next unchecked dependency-frontier item. Run affected focused cross-package checks after integration; the user's filtered three-tag gate runs every few hours; the canceled unfiltered run stays stopped.

Heterogeneous sparse/interval products preserve exact Hash/Sparse/nullary originals, seed migration, transactional publication and head assembly. Precision follows the actual absent/raw/sieve component shape, including the source rule that an empty union target adopts the input plugin. Fresh query heads therefore do not need the previously hypothesized bound adapter. Bound hybrids, arithmetic/mixed negation and infinite-domain explanation bodies remain open. Both explanation modes support configured checked LazySparse components; relation-level formula checking wraps only the finite sieve, while the fact-level formula guard remains. External condition-only/unsafe-head/negative rules and semi-naive SCC widening are implemented. Recent engine fixes include PDR inductive finite-frame model export, Spacer tail-only bounded search and Duality constrained lower facts. Absence of a Go caller alone never proves a live C++ path dead.

## 7. Acceptance gates

### Revalidated closure blockers (2026-11-02)

Whole-source reviews found these concrete remaining obligations; direct operation tests alone do not close their runtime integration:

- Relational selection: explicit `tr_sparse` and `tr_lazy_sparse` now select concrete storage and execute through the compiler, including mixed joins, recursive deltas and nullary results. Lazy query clones/publication use independent factory ownership. Configured lazy defaults share the context factory with compiler temporaries; interval, bound and uniform mixed sparse/interval public queries are integrated. Hybrid original finite/arithmetic/mixed programs, implicit product completion and broader plugin selection remain open.
- Checking wrappers: configured Hash/Sparse/Lazy pairs, source nullary Hash fallback, relation-level formula-checking finite sieves and actual checked DOC are integrated. Source-capable DOC programs execute typed DOC operations with an independent symbolic oracle; Bool fallback with nullary goals uses actual nullary DOC. Initially mixed Bool/positive-BV programs preserve their existing finite-checker route until concrete DOC/table product execution is implemented. Requested raw DOC with a checker favourite remains a separate unsupported combination; no whole-checker closure is claimed.
- PDR interface: source Boogie selection, `unknown` certificate text, owning `true` unknown answers and answer-export cleanup refusal have focused evidence; core answer/model/cover export and outer cover conversion now have retry-owner evidence; inner converter cleanup remains open.
- New PDR source audit: multicore-before-convex ordering is corrected and semantically tested. Duplicate noninitial proof states and post-resolution variable compression are repaired with independent proof-condition and original-premise checks (`1a05adaf0`). The DL/UTVPI term-ITE path now uses source blasting through an owning output reference, with vocabulary, equivalence, query and proof checks (`51c3bc755`). The old raw-return helper has no production caller and its separate lifetime issue does not justify an unused adapter. Source arithmetic-classifier parity is integrated in `5df01b4a5`: predicate arguments, Boolean function applications and quantifiers now select the same proof/arithmetic policy on initialization and updates. The empty-output interface branch remains a specific audit, not a reason to add generic ownership wrappers.
- PDR TRACE certificates now apply the live fixedpoint Horn model-conversion ledger to finite inductive models; false-result IF_VERBOSE deliberately prints the raw model. Sliced-query reconstruction, lazy gates and native conversion-refusal retry have focused evidence; this does not close the whole context audit.
- Spacer context: bounded status propagates through direct/expression queries and SMT2, early-false results simplify frames and perform configured validation, and completed solves honor `print_statistics`. Whole-source ownership closure remains open.
- CLP: independent audits close the live context/header algorithms and ownership, including enclosing preprocessing/parameter/backend teardown. Generic query wrapper references remain shared obligations.
- TAB: source-persistent selection cache/weight decay and clause sequence now survive direct and expression queries through one engine-owned session; query/backend/proof replay cleanup has retry-owner evidence. Inner subsumption/unfold/formula cleanup and live diagnostics remain open.
- The latest independent TAB method pass maps all live search/unfold/subsumption/selection and public query consumers. Concrete remaining work includes live IF_VERBOSE and TRACE sites, independent multi-premise proof validation, score cap/decay rollover and sibling-rule backtracking evidence. Dead `extract_delta` and unused `selection::is_reductive` remain excluded; stale generic cleanup descriptions must not justify speculative wrappers.
- BMC: live linear, quantified-linear and nonlinear algorithms are mapped; classify uncalled `nonlinear_dt` as reference-only, implement live diagnostics, and complete native cleanup refusal paths. Existing derivation-level binding and cancellation differences need explicit audit records.
- Spacer utilities/QE: live algorithms and legacy constructor rollback now have focused evidence. Coordinator cleanup after child result transfer remains open; `normalize(..., factor_eqs=true)` has live proof-obligation ordering and independent equivalence coverage. Uncalled helpers do not justify unused adapters.
- Duality and slice conversion: preserve partial status pending full method/owner records. The quantified-array proof reproducer now passes through nested-dependency cone preservation, positive quantified-premise representation and a structural derivation from the transformed source clause. Exceptional SAT publication requires proof closure, independently valid proof-checker conditions and source/background asserted-leaf provenance. The supported bridge shape is deliberately narrow; broader quantified premises and ownership auditing remain open. Unreachable partial-function producers do not justify new adapters.

These findings prioritize functional integration over adding isolated helper APIs. Each remains open until its source behavior and relevant end-to-end evidence are recorded.

### Final verification

- [ ] Every live source unit is implemented or has an explicitly justified Go/platform replacement; no unproven partial production unit remains.
- [ ] Every implemented algorithm has meaningful focused tests; add independent semantic oracles where source tests are insufficient.
- [ ] Public fixedpoint/API/SMT-LIB integration tests cover SAT, UNSAT, unknown and exception/resource distinctions for each applicable engine.
- [ ] Proofs are independently checked and their side conditions discharged; model/answer formulas are checked against original rules.
- [ ] Incremental queries, resets, engine switches, background assumptions and cancellation/resource recovery have regression coverage.
- [ ] Final source/ownership audit and manifest agree with implementation and documented deviations.
- [ ] Final native suite passes on the integrated final state; record the exact command and revision. Incorporate the user's tagged-suite findings before final completion.

## Remaining unit ledger

These are inventory obligations, not claims that the whole unit is missing. For each row: inspect code, identify live gaps, implement them, verify semantics and ownership, then update the manifest and this ledger. Paired headers share the audit but require their own interface review.

| `ast/ast.cpp:3146-3181`, declaration handling and API kind exposure | Constructs the supplied metadata or preserves its declaration; introduces no additional producer layout. |

No source producer constructs a single flat multi-premise node mixing forward and reverse pivots. ### Reach-selection invariant map — 2026-11-02

`spacer_context.cpp:165-198` wraps each evaluation in VERIFY and requires a selected fact via UNREACHABLE/SASSERT. The live case producers (`spacer_context.cpp:387-396,431-436`; Go `freshReachCaseVariable` and `AddReachFact` in `predicate_reach.go`) create Boolean nullary uninterpreted applications, including the unwrapped extension literal. These are constant probes, not arbitrary formula-evaluation callers.

The Go public selection probes report no selected fact as `(nil,false,nil)`. Each live must-select consumer rejects that state explicitly: `PredicateTransformer.OriginSummary`, `PredicateTransformer.MakeReachFact`, and `Derivation.CreateNextChild`'s current-state selection path in `derivation.go` return an AST-domain error instead of publishing a summary/justification. The no-false-case regression now also exercises OriginSummary's rejection for both true and missing interpretations and verifies no output or reference leak. This maps the source must-select invariant to explicit Go errors at the consuming boundary; it does not justify weakening any guard.
